# I&D Quality Demand Planning — Requirements Traceability Matrix

Generated by `node scripts/traceability.mjs` from [REQUIREMENTS-SPECIFICATION.md](REQUIREMENTS-SPECIFICATION.md), [TEST-PLAN.md](TEST-PLAN.md), [UAT-PLAN.md](UAT-PLAN.md), [SOFTWARE-COMPONENTS.md](SOFTWARE-COMPONENTS.md) and [KNOWN-ISSUES.md](KNOWN-ISSUES.md). Do not edit by hand: CI regenerates it in the release commit after every merge to main (CI-11), and the tests of that commit fail when it differs from the generated one, so it always matches the documents of the build that shows it.

| | |
|---|---|
| Use cases | 40 |
| User requirements (UR) | 37 |
| System requirements (SR) | 44 |
| Detailed requirements | 1007 (926 in force, 81 retired) |
| Test cases | 698 (25 retired, 138 UAT, 500 with automated L1 tests) |
| UAT days | 11 |
| Software components | 526 |
| Requirements in force without a test case | 0 |
| Trace gaps | 0 |

| UC-15 Back up, restore and recover local data | UR-11 | SR-15, SR-23 |

| UC-18 Explore the app with a synthetic sample dataset | UR-13 | SR-18 |
| UC-19 Build, verify and deploy the app | UR-14 | SR-21 |
| UC-20 Read the requirements, test plan and other project documents in the app | UR-15 | SR-19 |
| UC-21 Review backlog hygiene for the whole team: work left in ended Sprints, Features without DoD, unassigned work (Backlog Health) | UR-16 | SR-02 |
| UC-22 Decide which menu areas each role sees and what it may do, and assign roles to people by email | UR-17 | SR-02 |
| UC-23 Upload the existing QE mapping Excel and keep it up to date in the app — filter, edit, add and delete rows and columns — shared with the team | UR-18 | SR-22 |
| UC-25 See the month's key figures, exceptions, timeline health and evidence-based insights on one page beside the Dashboard, and queue the insights for the Monthly pack | UR-22 | SR-26 |
| UC-26 Open the app on my own picture — my hours against my capacity, my open work, my streams and releases — while the team's insights are shown only to the roles that lead people; look at Pulse Check over a quarter, half-year or year | UR-23 | SR-27 |
| UC-27 Bring the planned and recorded hours of the months before Time Writing in Azure DevOps — people who have left included — into the reports from the team's planning workbook, once, without writing to Azure DevOps | UR-24 | SR-28 |
| UC-28 Record my month in one place — add this month's tasks for my recurring work, enter planned and actual hours, add a new activity and see what is left before the deadline — on a page first tried out by admins | UR-25 | SR-29 |
| UC-29 See the team's month on one page — workload against capacity, delivery against Target dates and what needs attention — filtered by region, Epic and owner, on a page first tried out by admins | UR-26 | SR-30 |
| UC-30 Compare what the team had — capacity after leave and holidays — with the hours it booked, for the month and the year before, per person and per Epic, on a page first tried out by admins | UR-27 | SR-31 |
| UC-31 See the team's KPI tracker — an Excel workbook in SharePoint — in the app, read live in the desktop app and from the copy in Git when SharePoint cannot be reached | UR-28 | SR-32 |
| UC-32 See the QE and possible backup QEs of each DP release, and create the Features of several DP releases at once — with the right DoD and User Stories for their release type — assigned to their QE | UR-29 | SR-33 |
| UC-33 Create the Features of several Content releases at once, all assigned to one person | UR-30 | SR-34 |
| UC-34 Set up, per Epic, the extra fields a new Feature carries (e.g. Program, Platform) and their drop-down values, once for everyone | UR-31 | SR-35 |
| UC-35 See what is left to set up and what is not yet shared, and find any setting quickly | UR-32 | SR-36 |
| UC-36 Keep User Stories as reusable templates, tag them to Epics and Release Classification values, and pick them into an Epic's setup so they stay in step | UR-33 | SR-37 |
| UC-37 Set up a blank page of our own — CoNQ (Cost of Non-Quality) first — with the columns we need (text, numbers, dates, drop-downs, multi-selects), fill it in together, see it in charts and summary tables of our own, and keep it in the Git data repository | UR-34 | SR-38, SR-39, SR-42 |
| UC-38 Add a page of our own as a Tool Owner — a Table, a Table with Insights or an On-Demand Dashboard of KPI tiles, charts and summary tables — to any menu group that accepts pages or to a new group, without a release of the tool, and hand groups and pages to co-owners who run them | UR-35 | SR-40, SR-41, SR-42 |
| UC-39 Keep one master list of the validated tools — from the Tool Validations query, another query, a Windchill export or a list kept by hand — with each tool's validation date, validity period and due date, and see which validations are overdue or due soon | UR-36 | SR-43 |
| UC-40 Review a PI with the team: see the Features planned in the PI and read what was discussed on each of them in Azure DevOps — struggles, learnings, what moves to the next PI — in one place | UR-37 | SR-44 |
| UC-24 Prepare the two monthly Pulse Check slides (2x2 and Performance Pulse) for the month before the header month from the app's data, with the month's text kept in Git, and download them as .pptx | UR-21 | SR-25 |

## 4. Detailed requirement → test cases → UAT → automated tests

Ver. = verification method (T test, D demonstration, I inspection, A analysis); Pri. = priority (M must, S should, C could). UAT gives the UAT day of each test case marked UAT. L1 names the automated test file(s) of the test cases. Retired requirements are kept for traceability and need no test.

| Requirement | SR | UR | Ver. | Pri. | Test cases | UAT day | L1 automated | Issues |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

## 8. Trace gaps

None: every use case has a user requirement, every user requirement a system requirement, every system requirement detailed requirements, every requirement in force at least one test case, every test case known requirements, and every UAT day known test cases.
